        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

Fallback: the model may have written the JSON to a different file location\n+    if payload is None:\n+        schema_filename = STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES.get(step, f\"{step}.json\")\n+        for candidate in [\n+            plan_dir / f\"{step}_output.json\",  # Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. file → investigate → read file again → add findings → write file back.\n         \"\"\"\n         ).strip()\n-    return textwrap.dedent(\n-        f\"\"\"\n-        You are an independent reviewer. Critique the plan against the actual repository.\n-\n-        Project directory:\n-        {project_dir}\n-\n-        {prep_block}\n-\n-        {prep_instruction}\n-\n-        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}\n-\n-        Plan:\n-        {latest_plan}\n-\n-        Plan metadata:\n-        {json_dump(latest_meta).strip()}\n-\n-        Plan structure warnings from validator:\n-        {json_dump(structure_warnings).strip()}\n-\n-        Existing flags:\n-        {json_dump(unresolved).strip()}\n-\n-        {research_block}\n-\n-        {debt_block}\n-\n-        {critique_review_block}\n-\n-        Additional guidelines:\n-        - Robustness level: {robustness}. {robustness_critique_instruction(robustness)}\n-        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.\n-        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.\n-        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include \"FLAG-001\"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.\n-        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.\n-        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.\n-        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.\n-        {research_instruction}\n-        \"\"\"\n-    ).strip()\n+    return _build_critique_prompt(state, context, critique_review_block)\n+\n+\n+def single_check_critique_prompt(\n+    state: PlanState,\n+    plan_dir: Path,\n+    root: Path | None,\n+    check: dict[str, Any],\n+    template_path: Path,\n+) -> str:\n+    context = _critique_context(state, plan_dir, root)\n+    iteration = state.get(\"iteration\", 1)\n+    iteration_context = \"\"\n+    if iteration > 1:\n+        iteration_context = (\n+            \"\\n\\n            This is critique iteration {iteration}. \"\n+            \"The template file includes prior findings with their status. \"\n+            \"Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, \"\n+            \"and check for new issues introduced by the revision.\"\n+        ).format(iteration=iteration)\n+    critique_review_block = textwrap.dedent(\n+        f\"\"\"\n+        Your output template is at: {template_path}\n+        Read this file first — it contains 1 check with a question and guidance.\n+        Investigate only this check, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.\n+\n+        Check ID: {check[\"id\"]}\n+        Question: {check[\"question\"]}\n+        Guidance: {check.get(\"guidance\", \"\")}\n+\n+        Each finding needs:\n+        - \"detail\": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)\n+        - \"flagged\": true if it's a concern, false if not\n+\n+        When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.\n+\n+        Good: {{\"detail\": \"Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.\", \"flagged\": false}}\n+        Good: {{\"detail\": \"The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.\", \"flagged\": true}}\n+        Bad: {{\"detail\": \"No issue found\", \"flagged\": false}}  ← too brief, will be rejected\n+\n+        After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).\n+        Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.\n+\n+        Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for this check.{iteration_context}\n+    \"\"\"\n+    ).strip()\n+    return _build_critique_prompt(state, context, critique_review_block)\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

        Batch framing:
        - Execute batch 3 of 5.
        - Actionable task IDs for this batch: ['T3']
        - Already completed task IDs available as dependency context: ['T1', 'T2']

        Actionable tasks for this batch:
        [
  {
    "id": "T3",
    "description": "Create megaplan/parallel_critique.py with the parallel runner.\n\nMain entry: `run_parallel_critique(state, plan_dir, *, root, model, checks) -> WorkerResult`\n\nImplementation:\n1. Redirect stdout\u2192stderr ONCE before spawning threads, restore in finally.\n2. For each check, submit to ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=len(checks)):\n   - Write per-check template: plan_dir/critique_check_{check_id}.json via write_single_check_template()\n   - Build single-check prompt via single_check_critique_prompt()\n   - Create fresh AIAgent with same config as run_hermes_step(): model resolution (OpenRouter/zhipu/minimax prefix routing from hermes_worker.py:202-213), enabled_toolsets=['file', 'web'] (use _toolsets_for_phase('critique')), quiet_mode=True, max_tokens=8192, fresh SessionDB(), unique session_id\n   - Run agent.run_conversation(user_message=prompt)\n   - Parse using parse_agent_output() from T1, passing output_path=plan_dir/critique_check_{check_id}.json\n   - Clean via clean_parsed_payload() from T1\n   - Extract single check dict from payload['checks'][0], plus verified_flag_ids and disputed_flag_ids\n   - Return (check_dict, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd)\n3. Merge results from futures.as_completed():\n   - Concatenate check dicts ordered by ORIGINAL check order (not completion order)\n   - Union verified/disputed IDs with disputed-wins conflict resolution: all_verified -= all_disputed\n   - Set flags: [] (flags.py synthesizes from flagged findings)\n4. If any sub-agent fails, re-raise to trigger fallback in handle_critique.\n5. Do NOT update state['sessions']. Ephemeral sub-agents.\n6. Return WorkerResult(payload=merged, raw_output='parallel', duration_ms=wall_clock, cost_usd=sum, session_id=None).\n\nTarget: under ~200 lines.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing. {
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/parallel_critique.py\n+\"\"\"Parallel Hermes critique runner.\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import os\n+import sys\n+import time\n+import uuid\n+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\n+from pathlib import Path\n+from typing import Any\n+\n+from megaplan._core import read_json, schemas_root\n+from megaplan.hermes_worker import _toolsets_for_phase, clean_parsed_payload, parse_agent_output\n+from megaplan.prompts.critique import single_check_critique_prompt, write_single_check_template\n+from megaplan.types import CliError, PlanState\n+from megaplan.workers import STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES, WorkerResult\n+\n+\n+def _resolve_model(model: str | None) -> tuple[str, dict[str, str]]:\n+    agent_kwargs: dict[str, str] = {}\n+    resolved_model = model or \"anthropic/claude-opus-4.6\"\n+    if resolved_model.startswith(\"zhipu:\"):\n+        resolved_model = resolved_model[len(\"zhipu:\"):]\n+        agent_kwargs[\"base_url\"] = os.environ.get(\"ZHIPU_BASE_URL\", \"https://open.bigmodel.cn/api/paas/v4\")\n+        agent_kwargs[\"api_key\"] = os.environ.get(\"ZHIPU_API_KEY\", \"\")\n+    elif resolved_model.startswith(\"minimax:\"):\n+        resolved_model = resolved_model[len(\"minimax:\"):]\n+        agent_kwargs[\"base_url\"] = os.environ.get(\"MINIMAX_BASE_URL\", \"https://api.minimax.io/v1\")\n+        agent_kwargs[\"api_key\"] = os.environ.get(\"MINIMAX_API_KEY\", \"\")\n+    return resolved_model, agent_kwargs\n+\n+\n+def _merge_unique(groups: list[list[str]]) -> list[str]:\n+    merged: list[str] = []\n+    seen: set[str] = set()\n+    for group in groups:\n+        for item in group:\n+            if item not in seen:\n+                seen.add(item)\n+                merged.append(item)\n+    return merged\n+\n+\n+def _run_check(\n+    index: int,\n+    check: dict[str, Any],\n+    *,\n+    state: PlanState,\n+    plan_dir: Path,\n+    root: Path,\n+    model: str | None,\n+    schema: dict[str, Any],\n+    project_dir: Path,\n+) -> tuple[int, dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str], float]:\n+    from hermes_state import SessionDB\n+    from run_agent import AIAgent\n+\n+    output_path = write_single_check_template(plan_dir, state, check, f\"critique_check_{check['id']}.json\")\n+    prompt = single_check_critique_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)\n+    resolved_model, agent_kwargs = _resolve_model(model)\n+    agent = AIAgent(\n+        model=resolved_model,\n+        quiet_mode=True,\n+        skip_context_files=True,\n+        skip_memory=True,\n+        enabled_toolsets=_toolsets_for_phase(\"critique\"),\n+        session_id=str(uuid.uuid4()),\n+        session_db=SessionDB(),\n+        max_tokens=8192,\n+        **agent_kwargs,\n+    )\n+    agent._print_fn = lambda *a, **kw: print(*a, **kw, file=sys.stderr)\n+    result = agent.run_conversation(user_message=prompt)\n+    payload, _raw_output = parse_agent_output(\n+        agent,\n+        result,\n+        output_path=output_path,\n+        schema=schema,\n+        step=\"critique\",\n+        project_dir=project_dir,\n+        plan_dir=plan_dir,\n+    )\n+    clean_parsed_payload(payload, schema, \"critique\")\n+    payload_checks = payload.get(\"checks\")\n+    if not isinstance(payload_checks, list) or len(payload_checks) != 1 or not isinstance(payload_checks[0], dict):\n+        raise CliError(\n+            \"worker_parse_error\",\n+            f\"Parallel critique output for check '{check['id']}' did not contain exactly one check\",\n+            extra={\"raw_output\": result.get('final_response', '') or \"\"},\n+        )\n+    verified = payload.get(\"verified_flag_ids\", [])\n+    disputed = payload.get(\"disputed_flag_ids\", [])\n+    return (\n+        index,\n+        payload_checks[0],\n+        verified if isinstance(verified, list) else [],\n+        disputed if isinstance(disputed, list) else [],\n+        float(result.get(\"estimated_cost_usd\", 0.0) or 0.0),\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def run_parallel_critique(\n+    state: PlanState,\n+    plan_dir: Path,\n+    *,\n+    root: Path,\n+    model: str | None,\n+    checks: tuple[dict[str, Any], ...],\n+) -> WorkerResult:\n+    started = time.monotonic()\n+    if not checks:\n+        return WorkerResult(\n+            payload={\"checks\": [], \"flags\": [], \"verified_flag_ids\": [], \"disputed_flag_ids\": []},\n+            raw_output=\"parallel\",\n+            duration_ms=0,\n+            cost_usd=0.0,\n+            session_id=None,\n+        )\n+\n+    schema = read_json(schemas_root(root) / STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES[\"critique\"])\n+    project_dir = Path(state[\"config\"][\"project_dir\"])\n+    results: list[tuple[dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str]] | None] = [None] * len(checks)\n+    total_cost = 0.0\n+\n+    real_stdout = sys.stdout\n+    sys.stdout = sys.stderr\n+    try:\n+        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=len(checks)) as executor:\n+            futures = [\n+                executor.submit(\n+                    _run_check,\n+                    index,\n+                    check,\n+                    state=state,\n+                    plan_dir=plan_dir,\n+                    root=root,\n+                    model=model,\n+                    schema=schema,\n+                    project_dir=project_dir,\n+                )\n+                for index, check in enumerate(checks)\n+            ]\n+            for future in as_completed(futures):\n+                index, check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd = future.result()\n+                results[index] = (check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids)\n+                total_cost += cost_usd\n+    finally:\n+        sys.stdout = real_stdout\n+\n+    ordered_checks: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n+    verified_groups: list[list[str]] = []\n+    disputed_groups: list[list[str]] = []\n+    for item in results:\n+        if item is None:\n+            raise CliError(\"worker_error\", \"Parallel critique did not return all check results\")\n+        check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids = item\n+        ordered_checks.append(check_payload)\n+        verified_groups.append(verified_ids)\n+        disputed_groups.append(disputed_ids)\n+\n+    disputed_flag_ids = _merge_unique(disputed_groups)\n+    disputed_set = set(disputed_flag_ids)\n+    verified_flag_ids = [flag_id for flag_id in _merge_unique(verified_groups) if flag_id not in disputed_set]\n+    return WorkerResult(\n+        payload={\n+            \"checks\": ordered_checks,\n+            \"flags\": [],\n+            \"verified_flag_ids\": verified_flag_ids,\n+            \"disputed_flag_ids\": disputed_flag_ids,\n+        },\n+        raw_output=\"parallel\",\n+        duration_ms=int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000),\n+        cost_usd=total_cost,\n+        session_id=None,\n+    )\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Parallelize the critique phase by running each check as a separate sub-agent.

CURRENT: One agent runs all 4-8 critique checks sequentially. It explores the codebase, fills in findings for each check one by one, and produces one big JSON. This is slow (~5-10 min) and fails on models that can't produce large structured JSON (MiniMax: 75% failure rate).

PROPOSED: Split critique into N parallel sub-agents, one per check. Each agent:
1. Receives the plan + prep brief + its ONE check question
2. Explores the codebase as needed for that specific check
3. Writes findings to its own JSON file: {plan_dir}/critique_check_{check_id}.json
4. Produces a tiny output: {"id": "correctness", "question": "...", "findings": [...]}

The handler then merges all per-check files into the final critique_v{N}.json.

PHASES BY ROBUSTNESS:
- standard: 4 core checks → 4 parallel agents
- heavy: 8 checks → 8 parallel agents  
- light: no structured checks → skip (unchanged)

ALSO CONSIDER PARALLELIZING:
- Prep: Could split into "find relevant code" + "find test expectations" + "identify constraints" running in parallel
- Review: Could split into "verify criteria" + "verify tasks" + "verify sense checks" in parallel
- Any other phase where independent sub-tasks could run concurrently

IMPLEMENTATION:
- In hermes_worker.py or a new parallel_critique.py
- Use Python's concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor or subprocess pool
- Each sub-agent is a hermes agent with a focused prompt
- Merge results into the standard critique format

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must produce the same critique_v{N}.json format as today
- Must work with the existing flag_registry and gate
- Must be backwards compatible — if parallelization fails, fall back to sequential
- Don't over-engineer the merging — just concatenate the per-check JSONs

KEY FILES:
- megaplan/hermes_worker.py — where the agent is launched
- megaplan/prompts/critique.py — the critique prompt builder  
- megaplan/checks.py — CRITIQUE_CHECKS definitions
- megaplan/handlers.py — handle_critique

User notes and answers:
- We've made significant changes to the critique template system since this plan was created. Key changes: (1) The inline JSON template has been removed from the critique prompt. (2) _render_critique_template and _render_critique_checks no longer exist. (3) A new function _write_critique_template in critique.py writes a template FILE to plan_dir/critique_output.json that serves as both guide and output. Each check now includes question, guidance, and empty findings in the file. (4) The prompt references this file and tells the model to read it, fill in findings, write it back. (5) hermes_worker.py no longer handles critique template building — it just knows to read back critique_output.json. (6) _TEMPLATE_FILE_PHASES no longer includes 'critique'. Keep these changes in mind when critiquing and revising the plan.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-phase-contract: early-killed baselines pass truncated output to the execute worker, which may produce lower-quality diagnosis than full output. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-boilerplate: _run_worker() pseudocode hardcodes iteration=state['iteration'] but handle_plan and handle_revise use different failure iteration values. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-boilerplate: same issue as correctness-1: failure iteration would be wrong for plan/revise handlers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.
